->**HERMETIC PHILOSOPHY**<-
<note>original: "PHILOSOPHIA HERMETICA." Hermeticism is a philosophical and esoteric tradition based on writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus. In this context, it specifically signals that the manuscript contains alchemical or mystical secrets.</note>

A circular heraldic emblem or bookplate. The outer border contains the words "HERMETIC PHILOSOPHY" <note>original: "PHILOSOPHIA HERMETICA"</note> in capitalized gold letters. Inside the circle is a shield divided horizontally. The upper section features a white pelican in her piety <note>The "pelican in her piety" is a powerful symbol in both Christian art and alchemy; it depicts a mother bird feeding her young with her own blood, representing self-sacrifice and the "feeding" of the alchemical stone during its creation.</note>, pecking her breast to feed three chicks with blood, set against a blue background with golden rays. The lower section of the shield is brown and contains four stylized floral or cross-like motifs arranged in a square.

<term>Hermetic Philosophy</term>: A branch of knowledge seeking to understand the laws of nature and the divine through alchemy, astrology, and theurgy.
<term>Pelican in her piety</term>: An alchemical symbol for the stage of "rubedo" or reddening, where the matter is nourished into its final, perfected state.
<term>Heraldry</term>: The system of symbols used to identify families or institutions, often used in books to show who owned them.
<term>Ex-libris</term>: A bookplate or label indicating the owner or the specific collection the book belongs to.

<term>Hermetic Philosophy</term>: A philosophical and spiritual system that seeks to understand the hidden laws of nature and the divine, often through the practice of alchemy.

<term>Pelican in its piety</term>: A famous symbolic image of a mother bird feeding her young with her own blood. In an alchemical context, it symbolizes the "nourishing" of the philosophical stone and the stage of "rubedo" (reddening), where the work reaches its final maturity.

<term>Alchemy</term>: An ancient branch of natural philosophy that aimed to purify, mature, and perfect certain objects, most famously the transformation of "base metals" into gold.

<term>Hermeticism</term>: The broader religious and philosophical movement based on secret or "hermetic" writings that influenced Western science and spirituality for centuries.

## The Author’s own recommendation of the Art and the present Little Work.

It is known to the whole world that we have risen high in almost all professions and arts; just as <term>medical chemistry</term> <note>original: "Chymia medica," the application of chemical principles to the creation of medicine, often associated with Paracelsian thought.</note> has also reached such a height that almost all pharmacies are filled nearly entirely with chemical medicines. 

For what are all the distilled waters, spirits, essences, oils, extracts, tinctures, <term>magisteries</term> <note>original: "Magisteria," which were refined chemical precipitates or extracts believed to contain the essential virtues of a substance.</note>, <term>calcinated substances</term> <note>original: "Calcinata," materials reduced to a powder or "ash" through high heat.</note> and preparations (one need only look into their precious cabinets here and there) but pure chemical products? 

Yet it is still not enough; more is discovered daily, and something even greater could be found if there were no lack of powerful <term>patrons</term> <note>original: "Mecänaten," referring to wealthy supporters of the arts and sciences, named after the Roman patron Maecenas.</note>. Every wealthy man desires to become healthy and also to live a long life; but few are found who 

<summary>The author praises the advancements in medical chemistry, noting that modern pharmacies are now stocked with chemical preparations, though he laments the lack of wealthy patrons to fund further discovery.</summary>
<keywords>medical chemistry, pharmacy, distillation, magisteries, patronage, longevity, chemical medicine</keywords>

# IV

->IV<-

...might lend a hand to a capable student and assist them with their surplus means. People only say: “Oh! **Laboratory work** <note>original: "Laboriren," referring to chemical or alchemical experimentation.</note> wastes house and home; one hears enough stories of that, etc.” Yes, certainly, depending on how one goes about it. The shameful, deceitful, wandering alchemists who promise gold have indeed frightened and ruined great lords: but what does this have to do with **medical chemistry** <note>original: "Chymiam Medicam."</note>? Is health not the greatest treasure on earth? 

The great and powerful spend much on banquets, hunts, comedies, and balls—indeed, they often gamble away so much in one sitting that they could have secured for themselves and their neighbors the most precious medicines for health and a long life. Instead, through this wastefulness, they bring illnesses upon themselves and willfully shorten their own lives. It is the greatest wonder, indeed a <term>Lernaean Hydra</term> <note>original: "Hydra lernaea." In Greek mythology, this was a multi-headed serpent; here the author uses it to describe a massive, complex, and seemingly insurmountable problem.</note> of the whole **social order** <note>original: "politique," referring here to the management of civil society and the behavior of the ruling classes.</note>...

<summary>The author defends medical chemistry against the negative reputation of gold-seeking alchemists, arguing that the wealthy should fund life-saving research rather than wasting fortunes on frivolous luxuries.</summary>

# V

->V<-

...is that people do not take care to propagate the great treasure of health: while one speculates so diligently on the **art of war** <note>original: "arte bellica"</note> in order to kill people; the most beautiful, the most necessary study—namely, love of one's neighbor—is neglected. They say:

> "We have our **physicians** <note>original: "Medicos"</note>, who
> "study it day and night."

Those gentlemen **physicians** <note>original: "Medici"</note> who have no **practice** <note>original: "praxim"</note> in **chemistry** <note>original: "Chymia"</note> certainly study their **authors** <note>original: "Autores"</note> and draft **prescriptions** <note>original: "recepta"</note> from them according to their best judgment; these they give to the pharmacist, who prepares and pounds them in the mortar without further reflection **according to the instruction** <note>original: "secundum praeceptionem"</note> (which often neither the pharmacist nor the **physician** <note>original: "Medicus"</note> himself understands, as I have seen) and he worries no further whether the **physician** <note>original: "Medicus"</note> cures successfully or unsuccessfully through some **substitution** <note>original: "quid pro quo"</note> **from his prescription** <note>original: "ex sua praescriptione," referring to the dangerous practice of pharmacists substituting ingredients they do not have in stock</note>. # VI

->VI<-

...ends unsuccessfully: then the **doctor** <note>original: "Doctor"</note> excuses himself, saying he **collected** <note>original: "colligirt"</note> it from the best **authors** <note>original: "Autoribus"</note>; but he does not consider that the **pharmacist** <note>original: "Apotheker"</note> is often poorly experienced in **chemical** <note>original: "chymischen"</note> procedures. But what does a **chemical physician** <note>original: "Medicus chymicus"</note> do if he senses no progress with his medicine? He studies, speculates, and works until he makes his **secret remedy** <note>original: "arcanum"</note> or medicine **homogeneous** <note>perfectly blended and refined</note>, so that it can **produce** <note>original: "prästiren," from the Latin "praestare"</note> a desired **effect**; he investigates a suitable and convenient medical **subject** <note>here meaning a base substance or material</note> from the animal, vegetable, or mineral kingdom and turns it into a lovely essence, tincture, extract, **secret remedy** <note>original: "arcanum"</note>, **potent preparation** <note>original: "Magisterium," a term for a chemical substance prepared with its full potency</note>, or a **most sweet pain-reliever** <note>original: "Anodynum suavissimum"</note>, by which he strengthens and calms the troubled **Archaeum** <note>a term popularized by Paracelsus referring to the vital force or "internal chemist" that governs the body's health</note>, or spirit of life, so that it might be in a position to overcome its enemy, the disease, to **restore** health, to **preserve** it, and to **prolong** life.

# VIII

->VIII<-

"...spect and dangerous." I concede this with a distinction <note>original: "Concedo sub distinctione"</note>: *Against the power of death there is no medicine in the gardens* <note>original: "Contra vim mortis non est medicamen in hortis"; a traditional medieval proverb suggesting that even the best herbal medicine cannot prevent the inevitable</note>; and: *It is a fixed and irreducible fate for men to die once* <note>original: "Statutum est irreducibile fatum hominibus, semel mori"; a reference to the biblical decree of mortality</note>. It would be a great undertaking to defend the point that sometimes a **patient** <note>original: "Patient"</note> is himself the cause of his own death; in that he, in part, overindulges <note>original: "excedirt"</note> against the advice of his **physician** <note>original: "Medici"</note> and secretly follows ignorant advice, using harmful and **contrary** <note>original: "contraire"</note> things without the prior knowledge of his **personal physician** <note>original: "Leib-Medici"</note>. But that **chemical medicines** <note>original: "Medicamenta chymica"</note> are **dangerous** <note>original: "periculos"</note> and **suspect**—that has been sung for a long time, but wrongly <note>original: "sed male"</note>; one finds few **Galenists** <note>followers of Galen's traditional herbal and humoral medicine</note> nowadays, but rather purely chemical medicines. One should look at the **receipts** <note>original: "recepta"</note> and **prescriptions** <note>original: "praescriptiones"</note> of all the learned doctors; one should ask the **apothecaries** <note>original: "Apotheker"</note> whether the greatest part of their medicines is not chemical? The only issue is that, because up to this **date** <note>original: "dato"</note> no ab-

# IX

->A 5<-

...a **resolved secret remedy for incurable diseases** <note>original: "solutum arcanum in morbis incurabilibus"</note> is to be found in the pharmacies, and only such a thing could **vindicate chemical medicines** <note>original: "medicamenta chymica vendiciren"</note>. And this I have undertaken in this little treatise (just as I have already done in my ***Golden Chain*** <note>original: "Aurea Catena"; likely referring to the *Aurea Catena Homeri*, a famous alchemical text exploring the connection between all things</note>) to prove and **demonstrate through practice** <note>original: "per praxim zu demonstriren"</note> (**for a single demonstration is stronger than a hundred syllogisms** <note>original: "nam unica demonstratio fortior est centum syllogismis"; emphasizing that empirical results are more convincing than logical debate</note>); thus, I teach how all **corrosives** <note>original: "Corrosiva"; substances that eat away or destroy living tissue</note>, poisons, all **arsenical** and **mercurial subjects** <note>raw materials or substances derived from arsenic and mercury</note> can be made entirely corrected, transformed, and totally harmless—indeed, entirely **medicinal**, **protective against poison** <note>original: "alexipharmacalisch"</note>, **antidotal**, and even **pain-relieving** <note>original: "anodynisch"</note> against all pains—bringing peace **quickly, safely, and pleasantly** <note>original: "cito, tuto et jucunde"; a classic medical maxim attributed to the ancient physician Asclepiades</note>. If one labors and studies further according to this instruction: then the **secret remedies** <note>original: "Arcana"</note> will increase so much that the great lords themselves will **embrace** <note>original: "amplectiren"</note> them, as will the rich, who now regard them almost as nothing

He argues that once these "secret remedies" are perfected, they will be highly valued by the nobility and the wealthy.</summary>

# X

...value it, for they imagine it costs **immense sums** <note>original: "immensos sumtus"</note>; whereas I am confident that I could maintain a **public laboratory** <note>original: "publicum laboratorium"</note> for 1,000 Thaler <note>The Thaler was a standard silver coin used throughout Europe for centuries; 1,000 Thaler would be a significant but manageable institutional budget</note> annually, and a private one for 100 Thaler **including all expenses** <note>original: "cum omnibus expensis"</note>, equipped with a concise **pharmacy** <note>original: "Apotheke"</note> fully stocked with every necessity. Through this, in just a few years, one could **eviscerate** <note>original: "exenteriren"; here meaning to extract the essence or internal parts for study</note> and **anatomize** <note>original: "anatomiren"</note> all subjects useful for **medicine** <note>original: "Medicin"</note>, alongside many other curious experiments. Likewise, a **physician** <note>original: "Medicus"</note> can **perfect** <note>original: "perficiren"</note> his exquisite and **singular secret remedies** <note>original: "singularia arcana"; "Arcana" were specialized, often alchemical, medicines whose recipes were kept secret by practitioners to ensure their efficacy and value</note> for less than 100 Thaler a year, thereby gaining honor and great profit for himself. He may also **earn a place in heaven** by helping the poor, while establishing **immortal praise and glory** in the eyes of the world; for by these means, both the poor and the rich are **cured** <note>original: "curirt"</note> quickly and safely, and thus most would attain their recovery, etc.

# Chapter I.

## What Sort of Substance Antimony Is.

Although there have always been expanders of the medicinal-chemical realm up until Basilius Valentinus <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist, now believed to be a pseudonym for the publisher Johann Thölde; he is famous for his "Triumphal Chariot of Antimony."</note>: yet he holds the preference in this point, as one who undertook a manifold anatomy <note>In an alchemical context, "anatomy" refers to the chemical dissection or separation of a substance into its core components.</note> of antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, and after him Theophrastus <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), the father of modern toxicology and medical chemistry.</note>, and after him countless others.

Basilius writes in the old manner, that antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note> consists of a vapor driven together in the mines <note>original: "in mineris"</note> made of mercury <note>original symbol: ☿io (mercurio)</note>, a burning and fixed sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍re (sulphure)</note>, as well as a little salt <note>original symbol: ⊖ (sal)</note>; a definition which has remained until now and has not been further sifted <note>original: "cribrirt"; from the Latin 'cribrare', meaning to pass through a sieve or examine critically.</note>, though it has harbored this error until today. To now rightly untangle <note>original: "enodiren"; meaning to untie a knot or clarify a complex problem.</note> and sift this, one must go to the origin of minerals <note>original: "ad primordium mineralium"</note>, which is then delineated, as much as possible, in my *Golden Chain of Homer* <note>original: "Aurea Catena Homeri"; a highly influential alchemical book first published in 1723, which describes the interconnectedness of all nature.</note>, so that I the reg-

<meta>catchword: num; likely completing the word "regnum" (kingdom) or "regimen".</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the study of antimony, identifying Basilius Valentinus and Paracelsus as the primary authorities who first "anatomized" the mineral. The author critiques the traditional three-prime definition of antimony and directs the reader to his work, the "Aurea Catena Homeri," for a better understanding of mineral origins.</summary>

<keywords>Antimony, Basilius Valentinus, Paracelsus, Mercury, Sulfur, Salt, Alchemy, Aurea Catena Homeri, mineralogy, chemical anatomy</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 11 regarding the chemical origins of minerals. While the previous page established the authorities of Basilius Valentinus and Paracelsus, the text now transitions into a specific analysis of the "anatomy" of the mineral kingdom and its transition from a raw, acidic state to a fixed, metallic form.</meta>

...the mineral kingdom <note>original: "num minerale", likely completing "Regnum minerale"</note>, also when taken in its raw state <note>original: "crude sumtum"</note>, is called the <term>acid-rich realm</term> or **corrosive**, but which, after its decomposition, reveals its sugar-sweetness. Now one finds the primary essence of minerals <note>original: "primum esse mineralium"</note> through the crude anatomy of fire <note>chemical analysis using heat</note>: that it presents an <term>acidic vapor</term>, an acidic sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> with an arsenical <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> and aluminous nature, insofar as the mineral still remains in such a principle of arsenic <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, antimony <note>original symbol: ⚇</note>, and sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note>. More clearly, in common distillation <note>original: "in communi destillatione"</note>, it can be driven over or distilled in a more copious quantity <note>original: "copiosiore quantitate"</note> into such an acidic <term>spirit</term> or water. 

However, insofar as it is dried out <note>original: "exsiciret"</note>, concentrated, or almost fixed <note>original: "figiret"</note> into a <term>semimetal</term> or even a metal through **central heat** <note>the internal heat of the earth believed by alchemists to "ripen" metals over time</note>: it yields that much less of such acidic <term>spirit</term> in distillation. And if it is dried out into <term>talc</term>, <term>feather-white</term> <note>a historical term for fibrous minerals like asbestos or certain forms of gypsum</note>, <term>bloodstone</term> <note>hematite</note>, and other such **fixed subjects** <note>original: "fixe subjecta"</note>: one will obtain little or nothing from it without special manual operations and "keys" <note>specific solvents or secret techniques used to open a substance's internal structure</note>. Just as the sour or **acid** <note>original: "Acidum"</note> is the proper means to constrict <note>original: "constringiren"</note> or fix: the mineral kingdom itself shows through this that it is composed of nothing but acidic spikes <note>original: "spiculis"</note> joined together...

<meta>catchword: con=</meta>

<summary>This page explains that minerals in their raw state are acidic and corrosive but contain a hidden "sweetness." It describes how the Earth's internal heat matures these substances from volatile vapors into fixed metals and stones like talc and hematite.</summary>

<keywords>mineral kingdom, alchemy, acid, sulfur, arsenic, antimony, distillation, central heat, talc, bloodstone, spicules, chemical anatomy</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the "acidic" state of minerals. The author is moving from a general description of mineral formation to identifying specific substances—like antimony and vitriol—that contain a "loathsome" acidic vapor. This vapor is identified as the central challenge that medicine and chemistry have failed to properly address for millennia.</meta>

-> ——— <-

...is constricted and gathered together. Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note>, silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note>, vitriol <note>original symbol: ∞; here referring to acidic metal salts</note>, and antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note> testify to this, as do the ores of bismuth <note>abbreviated as "Wthi."</note>, zinc <note>original: "Spiauter"</note>, cobalt, and all <term>marcasites</term> <note>iron sulfide minerals often mistaken for ores</note> and metallic minerals that have not yet felt the fire <note>original symbol: △</note>; through distillation in a strong fire <note>original symbol: △</note>, these all give off an acidic, vitriolic-antimonial-vitriolic vapor and <term>acidum</term> <note>acidic essence</note> from themselves.

Now antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note> consists of such acidic sulfurous <note>original symbol: 🜍</note>-vitriolic <note>original symbol: ∞</note> fumes (I repeatedly use the term "vitriolic" with good reason, as it is found to be this and nothing else). For it is precisely for the sake of this <term>subject</term> <note>the underlying matter being discussed</note> that the entire purpose of all medicine—whether in the mineral, vegetable, or animal kingdom—resides. This is the **loathsome bride** <note>an alchemical metaphor for a raw, corrosive substance that must be "married" or purified to become useful</note> around which Chemistry and <term>Galenic medicine</term> <note>traditional medicine based on the four humors</note> still dance today, even as Chemistry and Medicine have ruled for several thousand years; this is the matter to which proper attention has never yet been paid. Furthermore, **antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note> consists of a combustible **sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> and, in part, a true **arsenic** <note>original symbol: 🜺</note>. Because this **arsenic of antimony** <note>original: *des ♁ii 🜺*</note> is not combined with sufficient sulfur and is "inverted," its raw state is poisonous due to the excess **arsenic**. Consequently, it causes nothing but **vomiting, diarrhea, and other dangerous symptoms** <note>original: *vomitus und diarrhaeas et cetera symptomata periculosa*</note> in the human body.

However, if this **antimony**, or its **arsenic**, is treated with sufficient common **sulfur** (which is indeed the true corrector of any and all poisons in the entire world), then all its **emetic power** <note>the ability to induce vomiting</note> is transformed and becomes compatible with human life, **without causing vomiting** <note>original: *sine emesi*</note>, as shall be more clearly demonstrated below.

The separation of the combustible **sulfur** from **antimony**, as well as the non-combustible kind, is well known and has been described by many authors. The separation of **arsenic** from **antimony** is also apparent, though it does not usually bear the specific name of **arsenic**. Instead, it is commonly called the **simple regulus** <note>the metallic heart of the mineral; from the Latin for "little king"</note> or the **Martial regulus of antimony** <note>original: *Regulum ♂lem ♁ii*; "Martial" indicates the use of iron (Mars) in the refining process</note>. That a **regulus** <note>the purified metallic heart of a mineral; from the Latin for "little king"</note> is a metallicized <term>arsenic</term> <note>original symbol: ⚠</note> can be demonstrated from this: if one **sublimes** <note>heats a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again</note> the <term>arsenic</term> <note>original symbol: ⚠</note> with soaps or **raw antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁re crudo</note>, or with an animal or vegetable fatness, or even better, treats it *by descent* <note>original: *per descensum*; a method where the substance is melted so that it flows downward through a perforated plate into a lower vessel</note>, it then presents itself in a metallic, reguline form.

One should place **raw antimony** <note>original: *♁ crudum*</note> into a crucible, cover it thickly with animal charcoal—burnt from bones or fish bones—or with vegetable coal dust; cover and seal it up and melt the <term>antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> beneath it: in this way, one will obtain a **regulus**. Now one may argue why <term>arsenic</term> <note>original symbol: ⚠</note> so readily metallicizes itself with animal as well as with metallic "fatnesses" <note>in early chemistry, "fatness" or *pinguis* referred to the combustible, sulfur-like principle within a substance</note>: by doing so, one will perceive a great advantage in **metallurgy** <note>original: *Metallurgia*</note> regarding the refinement of **arsenical** silver, gold, copper, tin, and lead ores.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on the relationship between antimony and other minerals, specifically focusing on how various forms of lead and the addition of "fatness" or sulfur can shift a substance from a glassy state to a metallic one.</meta>

Mix **red lead**, **white lead**, or **silver and gold litharge** <note>various forms of lead oxides used in metallurgy and glassmaking</note> (all of which are from the root of lead) with equal parts <note>original: *ana*</note> of **antimony** <note>original symbol: ⚭</note> in a vial or even a covered crucible; let them flow together by degrees <note>original: *per gradus*</note> of **fire** <note>original symbol: △</note>: in this way, one will obtain a yellow-red glass. But if you take only **antimony** <note>original symbol: ⚭</note> and **silver litharge** and grind half as much common **sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> into it, and treat it as before: you will find not a glass, but a metal of a shining marcasite-like nature, similar to a black **bismuth** <note>original: *Wism.*</note>. From this, one sees what the "fatness" <note>an alchemical term for the oily, combustible principle—the "soul" of a substance—that allows it to be melted or burned</note> can do, whether it be animal, vegetable, or mineral.

**Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> consists, as a mixture <note>original: *mixtum*</note>, of an **acid** <note>original: *acido*</note> of **vitriol** <note>original symbol: 🜔; likely referring to sulfuric acid</note> and a subterranean fatness or **bitumen** <note>original: *bitumine*</note>. Take the **sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> and temper its acid so that the fatness has the upper hand, and treat the **antimony** <note>original symbol: ⚭</note> or antimonial marcasite several times with it: in this way, you will express a more closely metallic nature. Melt the **regulus of antimony** <note>original: *regulum ♁*; the purified metallic center of the mineral</note> or metallicized **antimony** <note>original symbol: ⚭</note> repeatedly with soap: in this way, you will bring it ever closer to a metallic malleability <note>original: *Geschmeidigkeit*; literally "smoothness" or "suppleness," referring to the metal's ability to be worked without breaking</note>.

# Chapter III.
## On the Extraction of the Tincture and the Essentification of Antimony.

The previously prepared **glass** <term>vitrum</term> <note>specifically, the "glass of antimony" produced in the previous chapter</note> is now manipulated by **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, the legendary alchemical authority</note> in all sorts of ways to lure out its **juice and blood** <note>original: *succum et sanguinem*; these terms refer to the inner soul or medicinal power of the mineral</note>; and from this one sees how strangely, laboriously, and even curiously **Basilius**—and after him the multitude of other **chemists** <term>Chymici</term>—has attempted and tested this subject. Now there is a great point to be noted here: with what kind of **solvent** <term>menstruum</term> <note>a substance used to dissolve or extract the properties of a solid</note> one can best, most conveniently, and most harmoniously approach the antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note>. The **principle** <term>axioma</term> states: **"Like rejoices in like"** <note>original: *Simile simili gaudet*</note>. From whatever the antimony was born, raised, and nourished, that it will gladly drink again, as was noted before and will follow hereafter.

There is a dispute among most **authors** as to what kind of **homogeneous solvent** <note>original: *menstruum homogeneum*; a solvent that shares the same nature as the substance being dissolved</note>...

be adapted to minerals, in order to adapt them 
to the **animal kingdom** <term>regno animali</term>, so that they 
may **intimately join** <term>intime conjungiren</term> with human nature 
and allow themselves to **be communicated and mixed** <note>original: *communiciren und commisciren*</note> 
with all their powers **without any harm** <note>original: *sine omni noxa*</note>. 
It has now been proven above that the **mineral kingdom** <term>regnum minerale</term> 
takes its origin from the **constricting acid** <term>acido constringente</term> 
of <term>🜖</term> **vitriol** combined with alum—<term>🜍</term> **sulfur** combined with <term>◯</term> **alkali** 
(See the *Golden Chain* <note>The *Aurea Catena Homeri*, a foundational 18th-century alchemical text</note>, also *Pandora*); 
so it is also evident that **minerals** <term>mineralia</term> easily and willingly 
surrender themselves to **salty solvents** <note>original symbol: 🜊; likely saltpeter or common salt</note> <term>menstruis</term>, 
as to their own kind <note>original Latin: *uti sui simili*</note>; 
as one sees that the Spirit of <term>☉</term> **Gold**, <term>🜇</term> **Aqua Regia**, <term>🜹</term> **Sal Ammoniac**, 
the Spirit and <term>⚴</term> **Vinegar** of <term>🜔</term> **Oil of Vitriol**, <term>🜓</term> **Vitriol**, 
the **Mercury** <term>☿is</term>, etc., all easily reduce their respective 
minerals into their own likeness, namely to a 
**golden**, **antimonial**, **alkaline**, and watery nature. 
One sees it, I say, with one's own eyes, that 
they easily surrender to such **acidic solvents** <term>acidis menstruis</term>. 
But all these solvents are suspect and **poisonous** <term>venenos</term> 
(so say the ignorant), because they are all corrosive 
and **the greatest enemies of human nature** <note>original Latin: *naturae humanae summi hostes*</note> etc. etc.

I have also tested in the <term>Golden Chain</term> <note>original: "Aurea Catena"; referring to the *Aurea Catena Homeri*, a famous 18th-century alchemical text that describes the chain of nature</note> that in nature, strictly and in fact, there is no **salt** [⊖] **alkali**; rather, it is **Art** <note>The term "Art" here refers to alchemy or chemical manipulation</note> that first produces it, after it has driven out the **oily volatile acid** [Acidum volatile oleosum] through fire and flames, and has precipitated and fixed the more stable part into ash and alkali [⊖]. <meta>This page continues the technical instructions for a laboratory process. While the previous page established that "hungry earths" (alkalis) are formed when fire drives out volatile acids, this page provides the specific manual operations—melting, dissolving, and filtering—required to strip an earth of its salts entirely so it may be prepared to receive a new "spirit" or "salt."</meta>

...then coagulate, melt again, pour out, dissolve it again in **water** [🜄], and filter it: thus you will find some **water** [🜄] again. This melting, dissolving, filtering, and coagulating should be repeated until the **acid of salt** [🜒] is completely volatilized <note>turned into vapor or gas</note> and has flown out through the **fire** [🜂]: then you will find an earth deprived of all **salt** [†]. Now, give a **salt** [†] back to this hungry **water** [🜄]: thus it will become **gold** [☉] again, as soon as it receives its sufficiency <note>original: "Sufficienz"; the saturation point or the exact amount required to complete the substance</note>. 

And even more evidently: just pour a **spirit** [🜇] upon a leached lime <note>original: "Kalk"; refers to a *calx*, the powdery metallic oxide or mineral residue left after intense heating</note> that has been deprived of all **salt** [🜒], and see if it does not eagerly take the **salt of the spirit** [🜇] into itself and, with that, become a **water-** [🜄] or **salt-like** [🜒] substance? And so it is with the others <note>original: "Et sic cum ceteris"</note>.

Thus one must rightly understand and well distinguish between **salts** [†]; for there are **salts** [†] that allow themselves to be mixed together immediately; then there are **salts** [†] that conjoin with fermentation and noise <note>original: "Geräusch"; likely referring to the fizzing or effervescence of a chemical reaction</note>; likewise there are **salts** [†] that coagulate together and fix <note>original: "figiren"; to make a volatile substance solid and resistant to heat</note> into a **third salt** [🜒], etc. There are **highly volatile salts** <note>original: "salia volatilissima"</note>, **intermediate acids** <note>original: "acida media"</note>, and **fixed and very fixed salts** <note>original: "salia fixa et fixissima"</note>; there are lean, that is, we- <meta>catchword: nig; likely "wenig" (little/few) or "weniger" (less)</meta>

...[low] fatty **acids** [acida] <note>original: "nig fette acida"; in alchemy, "fatty" or "oily" often refers to the presence of Sulfur or a combustible principle</note>, which to outward appearance **operate in a contrary manner** [contrair operiren] to the previously mentioned lean ones; yet **at the center** [in centro] they are and become one. **For example** [v. gr. — *verbi gratia*]: the **vegetable substances** [vegetabilia], **especially** [praesertim] **salts** [†] that are **most volatile, intermediate, and fixed** [volatillissima, media et fixa], and the **fixed and most fixed minerals** [mineralia fixa et fixissima].

Observation also shows that the **most volatile acids** [acida volatillissima] do not **conjoin** [conjungiren] with those that are **fixed and most fixed** [fixis et fixissimis] **without an intermediary** [absque intermedio]; **for example** [v. gr.], if you pour **highly rectified oil of vitriol** [🜕 v. rectificatissimum] <note>original: "v." for *vitrioli*; sulfuric acid purified through distillation</note> into an **oil of tartar** [oo 🜖li] <note>A concentrated solution of potassium carbonate, which has a thick, oily appearance</note>, you will achieve nothing good; but if you first pour the **intermediary** [medium], **namely** [scilicet] a distilled vinegar, onto a strong

For example, **emetic wine** <note>original: *Brechwein* or *vinum emeticum*; a wine infused with antimony to induce vomiting</note> remains infinitely an emetic wine as long as it exists in its proper proportion <note>original: *in suo quanto*</note>. However, if one mixes this with four parts of other wine, it will indeed lose much of its emetic force <note>original: *vim emeticam*</note>, yet it will still cause the stomach some slight discomfort. If one mixes it with four times as much fresh wine again, its power <note>original: *virtus*</note> operates through the blood, **urine** <term>☐</term> <note>original: *urinam*</note>, and sweat at the appointed time and according to the illness. And if this mixed wine were then blended into an entire barrel of wine (as has indeed been done, once its harmlessness had been observed in this manner), then all that wine becomes medicinal and a **universal cure** <note>original: *panacäalisch*</note>. It will provide an **Antimonial** <term>♁</term> effect and powers <note>original: *virtutes*</note> for the preservation of health, without injury to human nature <note>original: *sine laesione humanae naturae*</note>, but with the greatest benefit to health <note>original: *cum maximo sanitatis effectu*</note>. Since **antimony** <term>♁</term> is coagulated from acidic, **tin-like** <term>♃</term> <note>original: *♃eis*; referring to the qualities of the planet/metal Jupiter</note>, and **solar** <term>☉</term> <note>original: *☉olibus*; referring to the properties of gold or the sun</note> particles and is concentrated to the point of dryness, it thus appears as a semi-metallic **liquid** <term>▽</term> <note>original: *▽*; the symbol for water, here used for a liquid or liquor</note> converted into a black-gray earth, whose **solar** <term>☉</term> liquid acid has been entirely dried out:

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 58 regarding various alchemical solvents (menstrua). ...others make **radical vinegars** <note>original: *aceta radicata*; highly concentrated, distilled vinegars thought to be more "essential" or "rooted" in their power</note>, and **radicals in many most diverse ways**. **As many heads, as many opinions.** <note>Latin: *Quot capita, tot sensus*; a common proverb suggesting that everyone has their own method.</note>

St. Paul says: **Test and try all things; hold fast to those which are good.** <note>Latin: *Omnia probate et tentate, quae bona sunt, tenete*; a reference to 1 Thessalonians 5:21, often used by alchemists to justify experimental practice.</note> Fioravanti <note>Leonardo Fioravanti (1517–1588), an influential Italian physician and alchemist known for his "magical" cures.</note> says sincerely: let it be whatever **solvent** <note>original: *menstruum*</note> it will, if it gives satisfaction and you produce a **healthy medicine** <note>original: *salubrem Medicinam*</note> with it, then keep it for your own. However, the artist must be endowed with his own skill, so that he himself knows how to make a **distinction regarding human homogeneity** <note>original: *distinction quoad homogeneitatem humanam*; the ability to judge if a chemical substance is compatible with the human body's nature.</note>; therein consists the **Golden Chain** <note>Latin: *Auream Catenam*; likely referring to the *Aurea Catena Homeri*, a famous alchemical book that describes the interconnectedness of all nature.</note>, there you have mirrors enough.

But before I proceed further, a new **alkaline author** <note>original: *Autor alkalinus*; a writer who believes health is governed by alkalis and diseases by acids.</note> opposes [this], who wants all **acid-absorbing subjects—such as alkalis, salts, or earthy substances**—to not be attacked with **vinegars** or **acidic solvents** <note>original: *acetis* or *acidis menstruis*</note> before they are taken into the body. [Basi]lius understands here a farmer who has not threshed for wheat bread, and he studiously <note>Latin: *studio*; with intent</note> holds back a trade secret <term>handgriff</term>. The entire art consists in this: that the antimony <note>symbol: ♁</note> (and all mineral subjects) be made into a vitriol <note>symbols: ⊝ or ⊕; a sulfate or crystalline salt</note> by means of <note>Latin: *mediante*</note> the volatile and acid salt <note>symbol: 🜔</note> of solar matter <note>symbol: ☉; gold or purified essence</note> and mercury <note>symbol: ☿</note>. In this way, it can then be brought into a liquid and saline state <note>original: *🜔-am*; referring to the salt form</note> through water <note>symbol: 🜄</note>; otherwise, it cannot. 

However, if one were to completely remove the salt of mercury <note>symbols: 🜔 ☿</note> or the volatile solar salt <note>symbols: ☉ *volat.* 🜔</note> (which has solidified <note>original: *corporifirt*</note> from the mercury salt on the glass of the mercury in the form of salt) with water <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, then much of the dissolved mercury <note>symbol: ☿io *soluto*</note> would go away with it. Then a dry antimony <note>symbol: ♁</note> would remain behind, which the water no longer attacks—or only very weakly—insofar as it still retains and catches some solar particles <note>Latin: *particulas ☉nas*</note> and remnants <note>Latin: *reliquias*</note> of the mercury salt. 

Therefore, when the salt—or whatever solvent <term>menstruum</term>—has dissolved the antimony <note>symbol: ♁</note>, draw it off until dry in a water bath <note>symbol: *B. M.*; Balneum Mariae, a gentle heating method</note>, or better yet, until it reaches an oily consistency <note>Latin: *ad oleitatem*</note>. Do not, under any circumstances, wash it <term>sweeten</term> with water <note>symbol: 🜄</note>; instead, pour the water over it after it has cooled, and you will obtain it correctly. Then proceed <note>original: *procedire*</note> as Basilius Valentinus says.

A lover of this art <note>original: *Liebhaber*, referring to a dedicated student or practitioner of alchemy</note> needs only to attend to this: that he first "corrects" <note>In alchemy, "correction" refers to the process of purifying a substance or neutralizing its toxic properties without destroying its medicinal core</note> the Antimony <term>♁ (Antimony)</term>, like other minerals <note>original: *ut cetera mineralia*</note>, of and from its arsenical poison <term>⚯ (Arsenic)</term>. M.</term> <note>original: *Balneum Mariae*; a gentle method of heating a vessel by placing it in hot water</note>, a **neutral salt** <term>Sal neutrum</term> remains behind. This salt joins itself to any acid or alkali, whether **separately or together** <note>original: *distincte et conjunctim*</note>, without any **bubbling** <term>Ebullition</term> <note>original: *ebullitio*; the violent fizzing or foaming that occurs during a chemical reaction</note>.

For, suppose that the **spirit** <term>Spiritus</term> <note>the distilled essence of a substance</note> is a most volatile acid <term>acidum volatilissimum</term> mixed with a small amount of a urinous or oily substance, or may even be called a most subtle **sublimate** <term>sublimatum</term>, because it burns very delicately in the manner of the stars. **For this reason** <note>original: *ratione huius*</note>, it **reacts violently** <note>original: *strepitiret*; literally "to make a noise," referring to the crackling or fizzing of a vigorous reaction</note> with the **greater acids** <term>acidis maioribus</term>, such as **sal ammoniac** <term>Salmiac</term>, **gold** <term>Sol</term>, and **Royal Water** <term>Aqua Regia</term> <note>a highly corrosive mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold</note>. 

It also reacts with the **spirit of antimony** <term>Spiritus Antimonii</term> and **salt of arsenic** <term>Arsenic Sal</term>, etc. However, it agrees more with the **lesser acids** than with the **vegetable acids** <term>acidis vegetabilibus</term> <note>organic acids like vinegar or citrus juice</note>. But if it is mixed with only a fourth part of a volatile, oily, urinous salt <term>Sal volatili oleoso urinoso</term> and is added to a **vegetable or mineral acid** <term>acido vegetabili vel minerali</term>, even before it is poured in, the acids already begin to smoke from its very scent. 

When it is finally added to them, they react together <note>original: *strepitiren*</note> through a **mutual and friendly sympathetic meeting** <note>original: *ex mutuo et amicabili congressu sympathico*; an alchemical concept where substances react harmoniously because they share a natural affinity</note> until the **joining** <term>conjunction</term> is complete. It is, as has already been stated, only to be observed—both with **antimony** [<term>♁</term>] and all poison-bearing minerals—that one should first make their **arsenic-like** [<note>original: 🜪-isch</note>] poison **antidote-like** [<term>alexipharmacalisch</term>] <note>From the Greek 'alexipharmakon,' meaning a remedy that wards off or neutralizes poison.</note>: thus all danger is removed, and they may afterwards be treated however one wishes. And no **substance** [<term>Subiect</term>] in nature does this better than the **neutralizer** [<term>corrector</term>] of **arsenic** [<term>🜪i</term>], namely **common sulfur** [<note>original: 🜍 comm.</note>], **as has already been stated above** [<term>vti supra iam dictum est</term>]. One only needs to neutralize the poison in the **arsenic** [<term>🜪</term>], then all harmfulness is removed. For behold: Take 1 **pound** [<term>℔ j.</term>] of **crude antimony** [<term>♁ crudi</term>] and ½ **pound** (or better, **equal parts** [<term>ana</term>], 1 **pound** each) of **common sulfur** [<term>🜍 com.</term>]; rub them together, let them melt in a crucible like water until the **sulfur** [<term>🜍</term>] no longer burns, then pour it out, and thus the **arsenic** [<term>🜪</term>] is neutralized; give 1 **scruple** [<term>Ij.</term>] or **half an ounce** [<term>Zß</term>] of that—

<meta>This page continues the author’s argument for practical laboratory work over theoretical study. If you go through the **written descriptions** <note>original: "ſcriptiones"</note>, you see nothing but mere **recipes** <term>recepta</term>. From these you conjecture that this or that recipe—though it be as long as a litany—will work, yet you remain in the highest state of uncertainty as to how you will succeed. But if you work and **labor** <note>original: "laborireſt," referring to practical laboratory work</note> on your medicines yourself, then you see tangibly that it is a truly divine help, and you may rely upon it with certainty.

NB. However, if they are in a **middle and temperate state** <note>original: "statu medio et temperato"</note>, they join with everything; they temper all **corrosives**, making them as sweet as sugar, and they make the **body** <term>corpus</term> <note>the physical matter of the substance being treated</note> trapped within the acids **volatile** <note>original: "flüchtig"</note>, so that everything rises over **essentially** <note>original: "eſſentialiter," referring to the distilled essence of the substance rising through the neck of the flask</note> during the process.

For the acids, through a **most friendly sympathy** <note>original: "amiciſſimam ſympathiam," an alchemical concept where substances are drawn to one another by natural attraction</note>, most rapidly pull all **urinous volatile salts** <note>original: "vrionoſa Salia volatilia"</note> to themselves and **saturate** themselves with them; they also coagulate with one another into a **neutral, vigorous salt** <term>Sal neutrum enixum</term> <note>A stable, neutral salt produced by the reaction of an acid and an alkali.</note>. <meta>This page continues the pharmaceutical instructions, specifically providing a recipe for a "cinnamon-red regulus" of antimony, transitioning from the theoretical authorities mentioned on the previous page like Basilius Valentinus to practical preparations and specific dosages for children and adults.</meta>

Take crude <term>antimony</term> <note>original: ♁ii crudi; the mineral stibnite</note>, <term>nitre</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔; saltpeter</note>, and <term>tartar</term> <note>original symbol: 🜃; potassium bitartrate</note>, of each an equal amount, for instance 8 <term>Loth</term> <note>a unit of weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce</note> each. 

Grind them well together, then add the mixture gradually into a red-hot crucible; let them melt together until the mixture no longer hisses and everything flows clearly. Then, pour it into a warm casting cone, and a glassy, cinnamon-red <term>regulus</term> <note>the metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible during smelting</note> will fall to the bottom, while the <term>tartars</term> <note>original: 🜃ia; the slag or impurities</note> remain on top. Separate the regulus and grind it into a fine antimony powder.

**The Dosage:** 
To young children, give 1 to 2 <term>grains</term> <note>a small unit of weight, approx. 65 milligrams</note>; for those 4 years of age, 3 to 4 grains; for an adult or elderly person, 20 to 24 grains. 

If one adds to this—proportional to the patient's age—1, 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6 grains of <term>Magistery of Jalap</term> <note>a resinous extract from the Jalap root, used as a potent purgative</note> or mixes it into a soft <term>extract of senna leaves</term> <note>a common herbal laxative</note>, it works very gently without any harm <note>original: absque omni noxa</note>, acting both "below and above" <note>meaning it acts as both a laxative and an emetic (inducing vomiting)</note> in every suitable disease <note>original: in omni competenti morbo</note> quite safely; it also works by itself <note>original: per se</note> without any additions. 

However, I have the patients drink water or soups (the more, the better) on top of it, nice and lukewarm. In this way, the stomach receives plenty of moisture, the medicine takes effect more easily, and the sliminess and <term>malignity</term> <note>the "badness" or harmful humors causing the disease</note> become more fluid and pass away more easily through vomiting without weakening the patient.

### Fol. 376. 

On this page, **Basilius** <note>Referring to Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist known for his works on antimony and medicine</note> makes a **secret remedy** <note>original: Arcanum</note> by sublimating **antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note> with **sal ammoniac** <note>original symbol: 🜹; ammonium chloride</note> and then separating the sal ammoniac from the **flowers** <note>original: Floribus; the fine powder produced by sublimation</note> again with water <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>. After this, he pours a strong **spirit of nitre** <note>original symbols: 🜖 🜔; nitric acid</note> over the flowers and dissolves them; then he distills the spirit of nitre over and mixes it with water, etc. 

Here one sees that he has jumped again to another **acid solvent** <note>original: menstruum acidum</note>, and indeed to one even sharper than the **spirit of nitre** <note>original symbols: 🜔 🜖</note>. But I make this secret remedy more quickly in the following way:

**Take:** 1 **ounce** <note>original symbol: ℥j</note> of the best **emetic mercury** <note>original: ☿ emet. opt.; likely a preparation of mercuric oxide used to induce vomiting</note>. Dissolve it completely in **spirit of nitre** <note>original symbols: 🜖 🜔</note> (it is better and safer in **spirit of nitre**). Add 2 or 3 times as much **rectified spirit of wine** <note>original symbol: 🜈 R; high-proof alcohol</note>. Digest <note>keep at a gentle, steady heat</note> until they are **united** <note>original: ad vnionem</note>; then it is finished. 

If one then adds 1 or 2 ounces of sugar, it becomes even more pleasant and does not need to be distilled. Its **healing powers** <note>original: virtutes</note> are equal to those of Basilius’s remedy. This secret remedy can also be specifically tailored with the <term>Panacaea vegetabilis</term> <note>a "vegetable cure-all," likely a multi-herb tincture</note>; this makes all **effects** <note>original: operationes</note> gentler and without **discomfort** <note>original: Incommodität</note>. 

**Likewise:** refine it and dissolve **rhubarb** <note>original: rhabarbarum; used as a purgative</note> or

Grind it all together, place it in a **retort** <term>a glass vessel with a long downward-pointing neck</term> with a wide neck, and distill it from a **sand bath** <note>original symbol: ∴</note> by degrees: thus a watery **spirit of sulfur** <note>original symbol: h🜏</note> rises, and almost no **oil** <note>original symbol: Ω</note>. **Why is this?** <note>original Latin: Cur hoc?</note> The **sulfur** **converts everything to spirit** <note>original: Ωirt; the author uses the symbol for "oil/spirit" as a verb</note> in the neck of the retort. <meta>This page continues the investigation into chemical reagents and their effects on mineral properties. Folio 391. On this page, Basilius <note>likely referring to Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose works were widely studied in the 1600s</note> distinguishes between the **extractions** <term>extractiones</term> performed with **sal ammoniac** <note>original symbol: 🜹</note>, **mercury sublimate** <note>original symbol: 🜵</note>, and **water** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>. He intends for the **sal ammoniac** <note>original symbol: 🜹</note> to **fix** <term>figirete, meaning to stabilize or render a substance non-volatile</term> the **antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, or rather its **emetic principle** <note>original symbol: ⚯, the quality that induces vomiting</note>, so that it should no longer cause vomiting. 

However, this proceeds weakly, because the solution with **sal ammoniac** <note>original symbol: 🜹</note> and **sublimate** <note>original symbol: 🜵</note> still causes vomiting. Indeed, he says himself that **spirit of salt** <note>original symbol: 🜅, or hydrochloric acid</note>, as a stronger **acid** <term>acidum</term>, causes the **antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note> to **operate through the bowels** <term>per sedes operiren, meaning it acts as a laxative rather than an emetic</term>. Yet, when it is taken in a larger dose, it nevertheless still causes vomiting. 

The **spirit of vitriol** <note>original symbol: 🜖geist, a form of sulfuric acid</note> is also a strong acid, but it breaks the vomiting power of the **antimonial** <note>original symbol: ♁io</note> even less; rather, it sooner increases it, or at least awakens it further. This is seen when one uses it to turn **antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note> into **butter of antimony** <term>butyro antimonii</term>, where the **mercury of life** <term>mercurius vitae, a potent white emetic powder</term> <note>original symbol: ☿ vitae</note> is **precipitated** <term>🜵irt, here likely meaning to cause the solid to fall out of solution</term> out with **water** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>. 

**Spirit of salt** <note>original symbol: 🜅</note>, **oil of vitriol** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, and **vitriol** <note>original symbol: 🜖</note> are also strong acids; of all those mentioned, these **fix** <term>figiren</term> the **emetic nature** <note>original symbol: ⚯</note> or vomiting essence of **antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁ii</note> the best. Now the question is, why do the aforementioned acids not fix or reverse the vomiting of the **antimonial** <note>original symbol: ♁io</note>, even though they are such strong acids?

But I do it more quickly in this manner:

**Recipe**: Take **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and **Copper** <note>original symbol: ♀</note> 6 *Loth* <note>a Germanic weight; 6 Loth is approximately 3 ounces or 90 grams</note>, and 1 *Loth* of **crude Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁ crud.</note> (or, if one wishes to have the **Redness** <note>original: R., likely standing for *Resina* (Resin) or *Rubedo* (Redness)</note> of the **Vitriol** <note>original symbol: ⊕; iron or copper sulfate</note> within it: then one must first melt the **Antimony** with a fourth part of the red variety of **Vitriol**, as stated above). Rub it all together, melt it in a crucible until it flows like **water** <note>original symbol: ▽; the author likely means to melt the metals into a liquid state</note>, pour it out into a warm iron spoon, and let it cool a little. While it is still warm, crush the **Antimony** <note>referring to the cooled alloy</note> in an iron mortar, place it into a vial, and pour the aforementioned **urinous water** <note>original: ▽ vrinosum; spirit of urine or an ammonium-based solvent</note> over it. This will extract a most lovely, sweet, and deep-red **Resin** <note>original: R.</note> very effectively—more so than with the **water** alone. Pour this off and add fresh **water** over it until you have extracted all the redness. Pour all of these extracts together: thus you have a noble medicine. **Basil Valentine** <note>original: Basilius</note> [writes] on its virtue on page 393.

**Take** <term>℞.</term> pulverized crude **antimony** <term>♁</term>, **nitre** <term>☉</term> <note>original: Nitrum; saltpeter</note>, and **common salt** <term>⊖</term> <note>original: Sal commune</note>, one pound of each. Grind everything and mix it together; add this gradually into a glowing crucible, and it will soon flow like **water** <term>▽</term>. When it no longer hisses, pour it into a warmed **casting cone** <term>Gießpuckel</term> <note>original: Gießpuckel; a metal mold used in smelting to separate the purified "regulus" from the slag</note> that has been soaked with wax. Thus, a **regulus** <note>original: König (king); the metallic heart or purified mass of a substance that settles at the bottom of a mold</note> falls out like a cinnamon-colored glass. The **salts** <term>⊖</term>ia <note>original: Salia</note> sit on top; separate these from the **regulus**. Grind the antimonial regulus and mix it with equal parts **nitre** <term>☉</term> and **common salt** <term>⊖</term>; grind and mix everything...

Grind this into a fine powder; the **dose** <term>dosis</term> is 4, 6, 10, 15, or 30 **grains** <term>gr.</term> <note>A small unit of weight based on the weight of a grain of wheat</note>, taken **by itself, or with theriac, or with another suitable carrier** <note>original: per se vel cum theriaca vel cum alio conueniente vehiculo; theriac was a famous, complex polypharmaceutical used as a universal antidote and base for other medicines</note>. However, he who wishes to impregnate the **panacea regulus with the power of gold** <note>original: regulum panacaealem cum virtute ☉; in alchemy, gold was associated with the sun and believed to possess supreme restorative powers for the human heart and spirit</note> should take from the beginning 4 **parts** <term>part.</term>

# Chapter IV.
## On the Regulus of Antimony.

---

On Folio 400, Basil <note>Referring to Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were foundational to early chemistry.</note> teaches how to make the simple <term>regulus of antimony</term> <note>The "regulus" is the metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible during the smelting of ores; here, specifically the purified form of antimony.</note>. This work is indeed well known: that when one draws the <term>Venus</term> <note>original: ♀; usually referring to copper, but in this context relating to the metallic "soul" or essence</note> of antimony upward with the <term>salts</term> <note>original: 🜔; likely referring to fluxes like saltpeter or tartar</note> and <term>mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; representing the volatile or metallic spirit</note>, the antimonial regulus <note>original: regulus ♁calis</note> falls to the bottom with a metallic luster. However, if too many <term>salts</term> <note>original: 🜔ia</note> are added, or if they are melted somewhat longer than is proper, it draws much of the regulus—or even the entirety of it—into the <term>slags</term> <note>original: Schlacken; the stony waste matter separated from metals during smelting</note>, and one receives little regulus or none at all. Yet, one can strike it out of the slags again immediately if one allows them to flow <note>melt</note> once more and introduces crude mercury <note>☿ crud.</note> that has been salted <note>original: 🜔irt; treated with salts</note> or coal dust into the mixture and lets it be consumed. As soon as it is consumed, it must be poured out; otherwise, the mercury becomes an <term>alkaline salt</term> <note>original: 🜔 alkali</note> again and turns everything back into slag once more.

<summary>This page provides practical instructions and warnings for smelting the "regulus" or metallic core of antimony, citing the authority of Basilius Valentinus. It explains how improper timing or excessive salt can ruin the process by turning the metal into waste slag.</summary>

<keywords>Basilius Valentinus, Regulus of Antimony, smelting, slag, alchemy, alkaline salt, metallurgy, chemical reduction</keywords>

--- Page 154 ---
<meta>This page concludes the instruction on recovering metal from slag and begins a new discussion on Folio 401 regarding the preparation of medicinal balsams from the regulus of antimony, specifically warning against the use of distilled oils for respiratory ailments.</meta>

...and in this way, more <term>regulus</term> <note>The metallic mass of antimony found at the bottom of the crucible after smelting.</note> is produced.

***

### Folio 401.

On this page, **Basil** <note>Basilius Valentinus, the influential 15th-century alchemist.</note> makes a **balsam** from it using **Oil of Juniper** or **Turpentine**; however, the **dissolution** <note>The process of breaking down a solid into a liquid state.</note> proceeds better if one mixes the <term>regulus</term> with half as much common **Sulfur** and allows the **Sulfur** to burn away. By this means, the <term>regulus</term> is **opened** <note>An alchemical term meaning to break the "seal" of a substance to reveal its inner medicinal virtues or to make it reactive.</note> so that the **oils**, by reason of their similarity to the **Antimony** <note>original: "ratione sui similis Stibii"</note>, can better attack and dissolve it.

**Note Well:** However, I consider it even better to use **liver of antimony** <note>original: "cum hepate Stibii"; a sulfurous compound of antimony known for its dark, liver-like color.</note>, because in any case, only the **fixed power** <note>The non-volatile, stable medicinal properties that remain after heating.</note> of the **Antimony** is required here. 

**Note Well:** For those suffering from **lung disease** <note>original: "Lungensüchtigen"; likely referring to tuberculosis or chronic bronchitis.</note>, it is said to be better if one does not use **distilled oils**; for they all partake of an **open, gnawing acid** <note>original: "acido rodente aperto"</note>—just smear an open wound with them and see what sort of pained faces the patient will pull!—and all **acidity** is troublesome to the chest and lungs. If such an **acid** circulates through the **passages** <note>original: "per meatus"</note> into the lungs, it not only increases the cough, but also helps to further consume the part that is already **ulcerated** <note>original: "exulcerirten"; referring to the sores or lesions caused by disease.</note>.

<summary>This page describes the chemical "opening" of antimony to create a medicinal balsam and offers a stern medical warning against using acidic distilled oils for patients with lung conditions.</summary>

<keywords>antimony, regulus, Basilius Valentinus, balsam, sulfur, liver of antimony, lung disease, acidity, dissolution, medical chemistry</keywords>

But **sweet oils** are said to be better, such as: **Almond**, **Poppy**, **Turnip**, **Anise**, and **Linseed oil**; for when one smears these into a wound, they do not sting, but rather **soothe** <note>original: "demulcirt"; from the Latin *demulcere*, meaning to stroke or soften.</note> and ease the pain like an elementary **balsam**. There are also more of this kind, such as **Linseed** and **Olive oil** <note>original: "Baumöl"; literally "tree oil," a common historical term for olive oil.</note>, which indeed carry a hidden **acid** <note>original: "acidum"</note> within them, yet one that is not so **openly corrosive**. 

There are also **animal fats** <note>original: "animalischen pinguedines"</note>, among others pure **butter** or **lard**; very noble is the gently rendered **Swine’s**, **Dog’s**, and **Human fat** <note>Human fat was a common ingredient in early modern pharmacopeias, believed to have superior healing properties for human tissue.</note>, and **Rose pomade**; for all of these contain no such acidity or sharpness, but rather possess a sweet, healing, and calming **consolidating power** <note>A medicinal property believed to help "knit" or heal tissues together.</note>, such as the lungs require; **for every acid is harmful to the lungs and the nerves.** <note>original: "nam omne acidum nociuum pulmonibus ea neruis"</note> 

Therefore, if one were to **digest** <note>In alchemy, to heat a substance gently over a long period to encourage chemical change.</note> rendered **Swine fat**—washed until it has no taste—with such a <term>regulus</term> until it reaches the highest **redness** <note>The "rubedo" or red stage in alchemical transformation, often indicating peak potency.</note>, and then [mix it] with such things and the best **healing agents** <note>original: "consolidantibus"; substances used to close and heal wounds.</note>, such as the herb **Comfrey** <note>Latin: *herbae consolid. major.*; also known as "Knitbone."</note>, **Golden Ointment** <note>Latin: *Vnguent. aureae*; a traditional yellow-colored healing salve often containing wax, oil, and resins.</note>, **Scabious**, **Yarrow** <note>Latin: *millefol.*</note>, and **Auricula**...

### Leaf 402.

On this page, he wishes to **anatomize** <note>To "anatomize" a substance meant to chemically break it down into its constituent "Three Principles": Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury.</note> **antimony** [♁] through its own principles, that is, through its own **sulfur** [🜍] and **salt** [⊖], which is a tedious, vexing, and not at all fruitful labor, until one brings together a **sufficient quantity** <note>original: "quantum sufficiens"</note> of the **sulfur of antimony** [🜍 ♁ii]. 

There is also a **physical reason** <note>original: "ratio physica"</note>, as has already been stated above, that all **minerals and metals** [mineralia et metalla], once their **salty principle of antimony or stone** <note>original: "principium salino-antimoniale vel lapidosum"; interpreting the shorthand ⊖co ♁eum vel Onosum.</note> has been dried out in the **bowels of the earth** [in visceribus terrae] by the **heat of the center** <note>A reference to the "Central Fire," a common early modern belief that a fire at the center of the earth drove the growth and maturation of minerals.</note> [per calorem centri], leave only a small trace of their **salty acid** [acido salino] in the **fire** [△]; since such a **specified acid** [acidum specificatum] is highly necessary in **quantity** [in quanto] for this purpose. 

Even if one were to obtain 1 pound or 1 measure of **sulfur of antimony** [🜍 ♁ii] according to the manner of **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a semi-legendary 15th-century alchemist whose "Triumphal Chariot of Antimony" remains a cornerstone of alchemical literature.</note> with great vexation, waste of time, and great expense, how quickly one ruins this! **Folio 402.** Here he wishes to anatomize <note>To "anatomize" in alchemy means to chemically decompose a substance into its primary constituent parts or "principles."</note> the **antimony** [♁] through its own principles [**principia**], that is, its own **sulfur** [🜍] and **salt** [🜔], which is a tedious, vexing, and entirely unproductive labor until one can gather a sufficient quantity [**quantum sufficiens**] of the **sulfur of antimony** [🜍 ♁ii].

...May, etc., prepared a **green butter** <note>original: "grüne Butter," likely referring to Butter of Antimony (antimony trichloride), a substance with a thick consistency often used as a precursor in alchemical reactions.</note>: then everything will proceed better.

**Folio 402.** He intends to **anatomize** <note>In an alchemical context, to "anatomize" means to chemically decompose or break a substance down into its primary constituent parts or "principles."</note> **antimony** [♁] through its own principles, that is, through its own **antimony** and **salt** [⊖], which is a tedious, frustrating, and not at all fruitful labor, until one can collect a **sufficient quantity** <note>original: "quantum sufficiens."</note> of **antimony-essence** [♁-♁ii]. ### Folio 444 and 445.

He speaks **in general** <note>original: *in genere*</note> of the **reduction of bodies into the prime matter** <note>original: *reductio corporum in primam materiam*</note>, namely the **Solar essence** <note>original: *Ens Solare*; the essential spiritual or metallic heart of gold</note>. For just as everything became visible out of invisible haze and steam; a visible, tangible water out of the astral steam; a **solid body** <note>original: *corpus solidum*</note> out of the **water** <note>symbol: ▽</note>: so shall this **solid body** return again into a **water** <note>symbol: ▽</note>, and this **water** can again become steam **through exaltation** <note>original: *per exaltationem*; an alchemical process of raising a substance to a higher state of purity or power</note> and thus a **primordial state** <note>original: *primordium*</note> or beginning; **and rightly so** <note>original: *et bene*</note>. Just as the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>symbol: 🜔 / *Spiritus Mundi*; the universal animating force believed to pervade all matter</note> lies hidden invisibly in such steam, as a very subtle **volatile salt** <note>symbol: ⊖ / *sal volatile*; a salt that easily evaporates and was considered highly active</note> (this, however, is well known) which, when it is **condensed** into **water** <note>symbol: ▽</note>, becomes a stronger **saline body** <note>original: *corpus salinum*</note>; and when this is then turned into **fire** <note>symbol: △</note>: it becomes fixed and fire-resistant, according to the **digestion of the center** <note>original: *digestio centri*; the internal "cooking" or maturation process occurring at the heart of the substance</note>. Now, that is the will of **Hermes** <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy</note> and the **Emerald Tablet** <note>original: *Tabula Smaragdina*; one of the most famous alchemical texts, describing the unity of all things</note>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical discussion on the "reduction of bodies," moving from the theory of physical dissolution to the spiritualized transformation of matter, drawing on the Emerald Tablet and the works of Basilius Valentinus to describe the creation of a "glorified body."</meta>

The **Emerald Tablet** <note>original: *smaragdina*; a foundational Hermetic text of alchemy</note>: "That the Above shall become as the Below." "**If its power is turned into earth, the glory of the world is complete, etc.**" <note>original Latin: *Si vis eius est conuersa in terram, completa est gloria mundi etc.*</note>

Therefore, **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings focused on the medicinal and spiritual aspects of metallurgy</note> intends that the vapor, steam, and <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: *spiritus mundi*; the universal life force believed to animate all matter</note>—which has been turned into earth (represented here as **Saturn** <note>symbol: ♄; associated with lead, the densest and "oldest" of the metals, representing the first stage of the work</note>)—should be transformed back into a salted **Salt** <note>symbol: 🜔; representing the physical "body" or fixity in alchemy</note>, **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note>, or **Earth** <note>symbol: 🜨; here likely representing the perfected mineral substrate</note>.

Such **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> or **Earth** <note>symbol: 🜨</note> should then be turned into a vapor and a spiritual **Salt** <note>symbol: 🜔</note>, if it is to otherwise become **compatible** <note>original: *homogen*; sharing a common nature or substance</note> with our human nature. This spiritual **Salt** <note>symbol: 🜔</note> or <term>spirit</term> should then be **regenerated** <note>original: *regenerirt*</note> back into a **Salt** <note>symbol: 🜔</note>, that is, into a **Solar body** <note>original: *☉sches corpus*</note>, so that it may become fixed and fire-resistant. Thus, body, soul, and spirit remain together eternally as a **glorified body** <note>original: *glorificirtes corpus*; a state of matter that has achieved spiritual perfection and immortality</note>; which body can thereafter penetrate and act through all bodies and places, in both fire and cold, without damage. 

And this is the most beautiful **symbol** <note>original: *symbolum*</note> for all believers: that after death, the soul will be able to take up its body again and, together with the body, will be able to pass through and penetrate everywhere. For everything that is salty, when it [is]

...just as it becomes the most fleeting spirit, it can likewise be condensed back into a fire-resistant, fixed, clear **body** <note>original: *corpus*</note>. Look at all **acids**, how enamored they are with the **solar** <note>symbol: ☉ia</note> **alkalis**, both **volatile and fixed**—indeed, how they eagerly penetrate all such earthly, porous, alkaline **bodies** <note>original: *corpora*</note> to make themselves physical!

In this manner, **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus</note> requires **Antimony** <note>symbol: ♁</note>, as well as all earthly **bodies**, to be **treated and re-treated** <note>original: *tractiren und retractiren*</note>; and this is nothing other than **dissolve and coagulate** <note>original: *solve et coagula*; the fundamental alchemical maxim of breaking down a substance to its essence and reforming it</note>! This is done so that the internal is brought out and the external is brought in, so that all powers may be revealed in such a way.

<summary>This page discusses specific alchemical recipes for refining antimony, contrasting the methods of Basilius Valentinus with the author's improved techniques involving "sealed earth" and crystallization with spirit of wine. The goal is to transform toxic minerals into "sweet," medicinal substances compatible with human nature.</summary>
<keywords>Basilius Valentinus, Antimony, Oil of Sulfur, Spirit of Wine, Butter of Antimony, Terra Sigillata, Distillation, Alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 190 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s commentary on the chemical procedures of Basilius Valentinus, specifically discussing the final stages of "fixing" a medicinal compound by removing excess moisture, a technique the author claims is shared by Paracelsus and his own work, the *Aurea Catena*.</meta>

During digestion, by the gradual withdrawal of the excess moisture <note>original Latin: *superflui humidi*</note> under the alembic and receiver, it is **coagulated** and **fixed**. NB. The chemists <note>original: *Chymici*</note> put one on very thin ice <note>an idiom meaning to lead someone into a precarious or difficult position</note> when they command that the entire moisture be coagulated. **Paracelsus**, however, states this manual technique clearly, as does **Hollandus** <note>Isaac Hollandus, a celebrated but semi-legendary 15th-century alchemist</note>, and my **Aurea Catena** <note>referring to the *Aurea Catena Homeri*, a foundational 18th-century alchemical text</note> teaches that the excess moisture can be drawn away; otherwise, much time and expense is lost in the **coagulating process**, or the glass vessel might even shatter. 

NB. Only the **salt-nature** <term>salinity</term> <note>original: *⊖nität*</note> should enter into the **coagulated mass** <note>original: *coagulum*</note>; for all **spirits of salts** <term>spiritus salini</term> become one body <note>original: *corpus*</note> or **Salt** <term>sal</term> with a body, according to how much **volatile salt** <term>sal volatile</term> it contains within itself; for the **refined water** <term>aqua recolata</term> <note>original: *▽ recolacea*; likely referring to the watery byproduct of distillation</note> will only with great difficulty, or most slowly, take on a physical form.

**Basilius** outlines the *virtues* and *application* of this medicine right there, and it is self-evident that this **compound** <note>original: *compositum*</note> must be an extraordinary medicine. To recount the **case histories** <note>original: *historias*</note> of its effects would fill a large book.

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>The author explains a critical laboratory shortcut for stabilizing medicines by removing excess moisture rather than trying to solidify the entire liquid, citing Paracelsus and Hollandus as authorities. He concludes that the resulting antimonial compound is a potent "extra-medicine" with vast clinical applications.</summary>

<keywords>coagulation, fixation, moisture, salt-nature, volatile salt, Basilius Valentinus, Paracelsus, Hollandus, Aurea Catena Homeri, distillation, alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for preparing medicinal salts and oils. The previous page discussed the "fixing" of substances and the theory of salt-natures. Here, the author provides a specific recipe for "Sweet Vitriol of Mars" (an iron-based preparation), continuing the commentary on the works of Basilius Valentinus or similar alchemical authorities. The catchword "pour" (gieß) suggests the next step involves transferring the liquid from the retort.</meta>

-> --- <-

The **Oil of Vitriol of Mars** <term>iron-based sulfuric acid</term> <note>original symbols: $\circ\circ$ ⊕ ♂</note> can be made in this manner: Dissolve **steel filings** <note>original: *limaturam chalybis*</note> with a good **Spirit of Vitriol** <note>sulfuric acid; original: ⚗ ⊕</note> and **Spirit of Wine** <note>alcohol; original: ⚗ 🜹 vini</note> mixed **as much as you please** <note>original: *q. p.* or *quantum placet*</note>. When all of it, or the most part, is dissolved: then precipitate it constantly and **successively** <note>gradually, step-by-step</note> with the **urinous water** <note>ammonia; original: 🜄 vrinofo</note> for so long until the **iron solution** <note>original: ſolutio ♂</note> becomes ruby-red, and no further; otherwise the **body of iron** <note>the metallic substance; original: *corpus ♂is*</note> will drop out <note>precipitate as a solid</note>, and the solution will lose its redness and become green. 

When the solution is now high red, **and indeed intensely so** <note>original: *et quidem intenſe*</note>: then filter it and draw it off <note>distill</note> or let it evaporate very gently **until a skin forms** <note>original: *uſque ad cuticulam*</note>, then set it aside to crystallize: in this way you will obtain a very delicate **Sweet Vitriol of Mars** <note>purified iron sulfate; original: ⊕ ♂is dulce</note>, noble and sweet upon the tongue. Collect the crystals and dry them, etc. One can also do this with **Copper** <note>original: ♀</note>, **Vitriol** <note>original: ⊕</note>, and **Tin** <note>original: ♃</note>; it also works beautifully with **Spirit of Salt** <note>hydrochloric acid; original: ⚗ 🜔</note> or **Aqua Fortis** <note>nitric acid; original: 🜄 F</note>.

**Take** <note>original: ℞</note>. Now this **Vitriol** <note>original: ⊕</note>, when there is **one pound** <note>original: ℔. j.</note> of it, finely ground, put it into a retort,

It emphasizes the precise timing needed to achieve a ruby-red solution before crystallization and notes that similar methods apply to copper and tin.</summary>

...pour upon it **one quarter pound** <note>original symbol: $\frac{1}{4}$ ℔</note> of a strong **oil** <note>a concentrated essence or acid; original symbol: $\circ\circ$</note>. ## Corollary.

But whoever wishes to reach this **Stone of Fire** <note>original Latin: *Lapide ignis*; a high-level alchemical substance often associated with transformation or medicine</note> more quickly, they should take my previously mentioned first or second **Butter of Antimony** <note>a thick, oily substance, typically antimony trichloride; original symbols: *butyrum ♁ii*</note> [prepared] with **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉</note>, **Salt** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, and **Water** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>, which has been **sweetened** <note>purified or neutralized; original Latin: *dulcificatum*</note>. Add to it his **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉</note> in the [proper] quantity **according to Basilius** <note>referring to the famous alchemist Basilius Valentinus; original Latin: *secundum Basilium*</note>, and **circulate** <note>a process of continuous distillation and condensation in a closed vessel</note> it until the **dissolution of the Gold** <note>original Latin: *solutionem ☉is*</note> is complete. Then draw off the **Water** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> until it becomes an **Oil** <note>a concentrated essence; original symbol: $\circ \circ$</note>, and add to this **Oil** <note>original symbol: $\circ \circ$</note> the **Crystals of Antimony** <note>original symbol: *Crystallos ♁ii ♁ii*</note>, and they shall be dissolved. **Coagulate** <note>solidify through evaporation or chemical change</note> it through **digestion** <note>prolonged gentle heating</note> and **fixation** <note>making a volatile substance stable under heat</note> into a most beautiful **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁em</note> and **Stone**, as I said before, [distilling] under an **alembic and receiver** <note>the cap of the still and the glass vessel used to collect the condensed liquid</note>. In this way, one may more quickly delight his spirit. For **Basilius** himself says at the end on page 456: **Take** <note>The traditional recipe symbol; original: ℞</note>: An **Oil of Antimony** <note>original symbol: $\circ \circ$ ♁i</note> and its...
